\Phi:G^{\iota}\rightarrow\left.\left\{(g_{i})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\in\prod_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}GL(V_{i})\ \right|g_{-i}=(g_{i}^{*})^{-1}\text{ for all $i>0$ and }g_{0}\in G_{0}\right\}

defined by
\Phi(g)=(g_{i})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}
is a well-defined isomorphism of groups.

(e)

The restriction of the adjoint action of
G
to
G^{\iota}
acts on
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
and, under the description of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
as a subspace of
\oplus_{i\in{\mathcal{I}},i\neq(2m-2)}Hom(V_{i},V_{i+2})
in (b), this action is given by

Ad(g)(X)\mapsto(g_{i+2}X_{i}g_{i}^{-1})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}},i\neq(2m-2)}

for all
g\in G^{\iota}
and where
\Phi(g)=(g_{i})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}
.

Proof.

(a)
\Rightarrow
) For
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
V_{i}
is the eigenspace of
\iota(t)
with eigenvalue
t^{i}
for all
t\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
. Moreover when
i,j\in{\mathcal{I}}
and
i\neq j
, these eigenvalues
t^{i}
and
t^{j}
are distinct. Because
g\iota(t)=\iota(t)g
for all
t\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
when
g\in G^{\iota}
, then
g
preserves these eigenspaces and we get
g(V_{i})\subseteq V_{i}
for all
i\in I
.
g_{i}\in GL(V_{i})
follows easily because
g\in GL(V)
and
g(V_{i})\subseteq V_{i}
.

Because we have
\langle g(v),g(v^{\prime})\rangle=\langle v,v^{\prime}\rangle
for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V
and our conditions on the direct sum decomposition, we get for all
v\in V_{i}
and
v^{\prime}\in V_{-i}
that

\langle g_{i}(v),g_{-i}(v^{\prime})\rangle=\langle g_{-i}^{*}g_{i}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle=\langle v,v^{\prime}\rangle\Rightarrow(g_{-i}^{*})g_{i}=Id_{V_{i}}

for all
i\in I
,
i\neq 0
. For
i=0
, we get that
\langle g_{0}(v),g_{0}(v^{\prime})\rangle=\langle v,v^{\prime}\rangle
for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
.
Consequently
g_{0}\in O(V_{0})
in the orthogonal case (respectively
g_{0}\in Sp(V_{0})
in the symplectic case) relative to the non-degenerate bilinear form
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
restricted to
V_{0}\times V_{0}
.

\Leftarrow
) If
g(V_{i})\subseteq V_{i}
,
g_{i}\in GL(V_{i})
,
(g_{-i})^{*}g_{i}=Id_{V_{i}}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i\neq 0
and
g_{0}\in O(V_{0})
in the orthogonal case (respectively
g_{0}\in Sp(V_{0})
in the symplectic case), then
g
is an automorphism. Because of the condition on our direct sum decomposition of
V
, we get easily that
g
preserves the non-degenerate bilinear form
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
, in other words
g\in G
. Because
g(V_{i})\subseteq V_{i}
, we get that
g
commutes with
\iota(t)
for all
t\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
and
g\in G^{\iota}
. (a) is proved.

(b)
\Rightarrow
) Because
Ad(\iota(t))X=t^{2}X
for all
t\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
when
X\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
, we have
\iota(t)X=t^{2}X\iota(t)
for all
t\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
. So if
v\in V_{i}
, then
\iota(t)X(v)=t^{2}X\iota(t)(v)=t^{i+2}X(v)
and consequently
X(V_{i})\in V_{i+2}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i\neq(2m-2)
. Note that this last equation means also that
X(V_{2m-2})=0

Note that if
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
, then
-i\neq i-2
, because
-i=i-2
we would have
i=1
and the elements of
{\mathcal{I}}
are even numbers. Because
\langle X(v),v^{\prime}\rangle+\langle v,X(v^{\prime})\rangle=0
for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V
and our conditions on the direct sum decomposition, we get for
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i\geq 2
and for all
v\in V_{-i}
,
v^{\prime}\in V_{i-2}
that

\langle X_{-i}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle+\langle v,X_{i-2}(v^{\prime})\rangle=0.

In this last equation, if we substitute

X_{-i}(u_{-i,s})=\sum_{q^{\prime}=1}^{\delta_{i-2}}\xi_{q^{\prime},s}^{(-i)}u_{(-i+2),q^{\prime}}\quad\text{ and }\quad X_{(i-2)}(u_{(i-2),r})=\sum_{q=1}^{\delta_{i}}\xi_{q,r}^{(i-2)}u_{i,q},

we get for
i\geq 4
that
\epsilon\xi_{rs}^{(-i)}+\epsilon\xi_{sr}^{(i-2)}=0
for all
1\leq s\leq\delta_{i}
and
1\leq r\leq\delta_{i-2}
and thus
X_{-i}=-X_{i-2}^{T}
.

If
i=2
and we are in the orthogonal case, from
\langle X_{-2}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle+\langle v,X_{0}(v^{\prime})\rangle=0
for all
v\in V_{-2}
,
v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
and writing

X_{-2}(u_{-2,s})=\sum_{q^{\prime}=1}^{\delta_{0}}\xi_{q^{\prime},s}^{(-2)}u_{0,q^{\prime}}\quad\text{ and }\quad X_{0}(u_{0,r})=\sum_{q=1}^{\delta_{2}}\xi_{q,r}^{(0)}u_{2,q},

we get that
\xi_{(\delta_{0}+1-r),s}^{(-2)}+\xi_{s,r}^{(0)}=0
for all
1\leq r\leq\delta_{0}
,
1\leq s\leq\delta_{2}
and
X_{-2}=-JX_{0}^{T}
.

If
i=2
and we are in the symplectic case, from
\langle X_{-2}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle+\langle v,X_{0}(v^{\prime})\rangle=0
for all
v\in V_{-2}
,
v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
and writing

X_{-2}(u_{-2,s})=\sum_{q^{\prime}=1}^{\delta^{\prime}_{0}}\xi_{-q^{\prime},s}^{(-2)}u_{0,-q^{\prime}}+\sum_{q^{\prime\prime}=1}^{\delta^{\prime}_{0}}\xi_{q^{\prime\prime},s}^{(-2)}u_{0,q^{\prime\prime}}\quad\text{ and }\quad X_{0}(u_{0,r})=\sum_{q=1}^{\delta_{2}}\xi_{q,r}^{(0)}u_{2,q},

for
s=1,2,\dots,\delta_{2}
and
r=-\delta^{\prime}_{0},\dots,-2,-1,1,2,\dots,\delta^{\prime}_{0}
, we get that

\begin{cases}\xi_{-r,s}^{(-2)}-\xi_{s,r}^{(0)}=0&\text{if $r<0$;}\\
\\
-\xi_{-r,s}^{(-2)}-\xi_{s,r}^{(0)}=0&\text{if $r>0$.}\end{cases}

Consequently

X_{-2}=\begin{pmatrix}X_{-2}^{+}\\
X_{-2}^{-}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\phantom{-}(X_{0}^{-})^{T}\\
-(X_{0}^{+})^{T}\end{pmatrix}\text{ where }X_{0}=(X_{0}^{+},X_{0}^{-}).

\Leftarrow
) If
X_{-i}=-X_{i-2}^{T}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i\geq 4
, and in the orthogonal case, we have
X_{-2}=-X_{0}^{T}
; while in the symplectic case, we have

X_{0}=(X_{0}^{+},X_{0}^{-})\quad\text{ and }X_{-2}=\begin{pmatrix}X_{-2}^{+}\\
X_{-2}^{-}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\phantom{-}(X_{0}^{-})^{T}\\
-(X_{0}^{+})^{T}\end{pmatrix},

with the above notation, then we get that
\langle X(v),v^{\prime}\rangle+\langle v,X(v^{\prime})\rangle=0
for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V
by only checking it on the basis
{\mathcal{B}}
and this means that
X\in{\mathfrak{g}}
. If we add the condition
X(V_{i})\subseteq V_{i+2}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i\neq(2m-2)
, we have that
X\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
. The rest of the statements in (b) are easily proved.

(c)
\Rightarrow
) Because
Ad(\iota(t))Z=Z
for all
t\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
when
Z\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
, we have
\iota(t)Z=Z\iota(t)
for all
t\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}
. So if
v\in V_{i}
, then
\iota(t)Z(v)=Z\iota(t)(v)=t^{i}Z(v)
and consequently
Z(V_{i})\in V_{i}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
.

Because
\langle Z(v),v^{\prime}\rangle+\langle v,Z(v^{\prime})\rangle=0
for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V
and our conditions on the direct sum decomposition, we get for
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
and for all
v\in V_{i}
,
v^{\prime}\in V_{-i}
that

\langle Z_{i}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle+\langle v,Z_{-i}(v^{\prime})\rangle=0.

In this last equation, if we substitute

Z_{i}(u_{i,s})=\sum_{q^{\prime}=1}^{\delta_{i}}\zeta_{q^{\prime},s}^{(i)}u_{i,q^{\prime}}\quad\text{ and }\quad Z_{-i}(u_{-i,r})=\sum_{q=1}^{\delta_{i}}\zeta_{q,r}^{(-i)}u_{-i,q},

we get for
i\geq 2
that
\zeta_{r,s}^{(i)}+\zeta_{s,r}^{(-i)}=0
for all
1\leq s,r\leq\delta_{i}
. Thus
Z_{-i}=-Z_{i}^{T}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
and
i\geq 2
.

For
i=0
, we get that

\langle Z_{0}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle+\langle v,Z_{0}(v^{\prime})\rangle=0\quad\text{ for all $v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}$,}

in other words,
Z_{0}
is in the Lie algebra corresponding to
V_{0}
and the bilinear form
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
.

\Leftarrow
) If
Z_{-i}=-Z_{i}^{T}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i\geq 2
and if
Z_{0}
is in the Lie algebra corresponding to
V_{0}
and the bilinear form
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
, then we get that
\langle Z(v),v^{\prime}\rangle+\langle v,Z(v^{\prime})\rangle=0
for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V
by only checking it on the basis
{\mathcal{B}}
and this means that
Z\in{\mathfrak{g}}
. If we add the condition
Z(V_{i})\subseteq V_{i}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
, we have that
Z\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
. The rest of the statements in (c) are easily proved.

(d) follows easily from (a).

(e) follows easily from (b) and the fact that
Ad(g)(X)=gXg^{-1}
for all
g\in G^{\iota}
.
∎

Corollary 4.4
.

The dimension
\dim({\mathfrak{g}}_{2})
of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
is equal to

\sum_{i\in{\mathcal{I}},0\leq i<(2m-2)}\delta_{i}\delta_{i+2}

Proof.

This follows easily from proposition
4.3
(b).
∎

4.5.

In (b) and (e) above, we have shown that
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
with the action of the restriction of the adjoint action of
G
to
G^{\iota}
is isomorphic to the space of orthogonal (respectively symplectic) representations of the symmetric quiver of type
A_{m}^{odd}
.

4.6.

The notions of symmetric function
c:{\mathbf{B}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
, of associated coefficient function
{\mathbf{c}}:{\mathbf{B}}/\tau\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
and dimension vector
\delta({\mathbf{c}})=\delta(c)
for
{\mathbf{c}}
and
c
are identical to the ones when
{\mathcal{I}}
is even as in definition
3.4
.

{\mathfrak{C}}
and
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\delta}
for
\delta=(\delta_{i})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{I}}
such that
\delta_{i}=\delta_{-i}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
denote respectively sets of coefficient functions as in notation
3.5
.

Notation 4.7
.

Let
\delta=(\delta_{i})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}\in{\mathbb{N}}^{\mathcal{I}}
be a vector such that
\delta_{-i}=\delta_{i}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
and let
{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{\delta}
be a coefficient function. Denote by
V({\mathbf{c}})
: the direct sum as
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded vector space and as
\epsilon
-representations of
{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}})
copies of
V({\mathcal{O}})
with a
{\mathcal{I}}
-basis

{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathbf{c}}=\coprod_{{\mathcal{O}}\in{\mathbf{B}}/\tau}\coprod_{b\in{\mathcal{O}}}\{v_{i}^{j}(b)\mid i\in Supp(b),1\leq j\leq{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}})\}

such that, for
b=b(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1})
,
b^{\prime}=b(i_{2},j_{2},k_{2})
,
i,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
, we have

\langle v_{i}^{j}(b),v_{i^{\prime}}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $j^{\prime}=j$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$, $i>0$ and $i+i^{\prime}=0$; }\\
\epsilon,&\text{if $j^{\prime}=j$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$, $i<0$ and $i+i^{\prime}=0$; }\\
1,&\text{if $j^{\prime}=j$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$, $i=i^{\prime}=0$ and $b$ is above}\\
&\text{the principal diagonal; }\\
\epsilon,&\text{if $j^{\prime}=j$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$, $i=i^{\prime}=0$ and $b$ is below}\\
&\text{the principal diagonal; }\\
\epsilon^{k_{1}},&\text{if $j^{\prime}=j$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$, $\tau(b)\neq b$, $i=i^{\prime}=0$ and $b$ is on}\\
&\text{the principal diagonal; }\\
1,&\text{if $j^{\prime}=j$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)=b$, $i=i^{\prime}=0$ and $b$ is on}\\
&\text{the principal diagonal; }\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

This is just from the formulae of
2.11
adapted to our situation.

We will denote by
{\mathfrak{g}}(V({\mathbf{c}}))
: the Lie algebra corresponding to
(V({\mathbf{c}}),\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\mathbf{c}})
. In other words,
{\mathfrak{g}}(V({\mathbf{c}}))
is the vector space of linear transformations
X:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V({\mathbf{c}})
such that
\langle X(u),v\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle u,X(v)\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0
for all
u,v\in V({\mathbf{c}})
.

From our construction and proved similarly as in notation
3.6
, we have that
V({\mathbf{c}})=\oplus_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}V_{i}({\mathbf{c}})
and
\dim(V_{i}({\mathbf{c}}))=\delta_{i}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
.

Let the three
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded linear transformations

E_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V({\mathbf{c}}),\quad H_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V({\mathbf{c}})\quad\text{ and }\quad F_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V({\mathbf{c}})

denote the direct sum as
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded linear transformation of
{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}})
copies of the linear transformations

E_{\mathcal{O}}:V({\mathcal{O}})\rightarrow V({\mathcal{O}}),\quad H_{\mathcal{O}}:V({\mathcal{O}})\rightarrow V({\mathcal{O}})\quad\text{ and }\quad F_{\mathcal{O}}:V({\mathcal{O}})\rightarrow V({\mathcal{O}}).

By applying proposition
2.16
on each summand, we get that these three linear transformations are such that

•

E_{\mathbf{c}}
is of degree
2
,
H_{\mathbf{c}}
is of degree
0
and
F_{\mathbf{c}}
is of degree
-2
;

•

E_{\mathbf{c}},H_{\mathbf{c}},F_{\mathbf{c}}
belong to the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}(V({\mathbf{c}}))
;

•

[H_{\mathbf{c}},E_{\mathbf{c}}]=2E_{\mathbf{c}}
,
[H_{\mathbf{c}},F_{\mathbf{c}}]=-2F_{\mathbf{c}}
and
[E_{\mathbf{c}},F_{\mathbf{c}}]=H_{\mathbf{c}}
, in other words, the triple
(E_{\mathbf{c}},H_{\mathbf{c}},F_{\mathbf{c}})
is an
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded Jacobson-Morozov triple for the Lie algebra of
(V({\mathbf{c}}),\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\mathbf{c}})
;

•

the
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded linear transformation
\phi_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V({\mathbf{c}})
given by

\phi_{{\mathbf{c}},i}={E_{\mathbf{c}}}|_{V_{i}({\mathbf{c}})}:V_{i}({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V_{i+2}({\mathbf{c}})\quad\text{for all $i\in{\mathcal{I}}$ such that $(i+2)\in{\mathcal{I}}$},

is such that
(V({\mathbf{c}}),\phi_{\mathbf{c}},\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\mathbf{c}})
is an
\epsilon
-representation.

As such we can express these linear transformations on the elements of the basis
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathbf{c}}
using the expressions in
2.15
. For example, let
{\mathcal{O}}
denote the
\langle\tau\rangle
-orbit of
b
, then, if the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}
has no overlapping
{\mathcal{I}}
-box supports,
1\leq j\leq{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}})
and
i\in Supp(b)
, we have

E_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{j}(b))=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{ if $i=\max(Supp(b))$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}v_{i+2}^{j}(b),&\text{ if $i\neq\max(Supp(b))$ and $i>0$;}\\
-v_{i+2}^{j}(b),&\text{ if $i\neq\max(Supp(b))$ and $i<0$;}\end{cases}

while if the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}
has overlapping
{\mathcal{I}}
-box supports,
b=b(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1})\in{\mathcal{O}}
,
1\leq j\leq{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}})
and
i\in Supp(b)
, then we have

E_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{j}(b))=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{if $i=\max(Supp(b))$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}v_{i+2}^{j}(b),&\text{if $i\neq\max(Supp(b))$ and $i>0$;}\\
-v_{i+2}^{j}(b),&\text{if $i<0$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}\epsilon v_{2}^{j}(b),&\text{if $i=0$, $\max(Supp(b))>0$ and $b$ is below the principal}\\
&\text{diagonal;}\\
{\phantom{-}}v_{2}^{j}(b),&\text{if $i=0$, $\max(Supp(b))>0$ and $b$ is above the principal}\\
&\text{diagonal;}\\
{\phantom{-}}\epsilon^{k_{1}}v_{2}^{j}(b),&\text{if $i=0$, $\max(Supp(b))>0$ and $b$ is on the principal}\\
&\text{diagonal;}\end{cases}

4.8.

Given an element
X\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
, we will now associate to it a coefficient function
{\mathbf{c}}_{X}:{\mathbf{B}}/\tau\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
as follows. By lemma
4.3
(b) and with the notation of this lemma,
X\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
is equivalent to giving the
\epsilon
-representation
(V,\phi_{V},\langle\ ,\ \rangle)
, where

\phi_{V,i}=X|_{V_{i}}:V_{i}\rightarrow V_{i+2}\quad v\mapsto X(v)\quad\text{ for all $v\in V_{i}$}

is the restriction of
X
on
V_{i}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i\neq(2m-1)
. By the theorem of Krull-Remak-Schmidt, this
\epsilon
-representation can be written in essentially unique way as a direct sum of indecomposable
\epsilon
-representations (up to isomorphism). We saw in proposition
2.16
that the set of indecomposable
\epsilon
-representations is in bijection with the set
{\mathbf{B}}/\tau
. More precisely, for each orbit
{\mathcal{O}}\in{\mathbf{B}}/\tau
, we have constructed a representative
(V({\mathcal{O}}),\phi_{\mathcal{O}},\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\mathcal{O}})
of the isomorphism class of the indecomposable
\epsilon
-representation. Denote by
{\mathbf{c}}_{X}({\mathcal{O}})
: the multiplicity (up to isomorphism) of the indecomposable
\epsilon
-representation
(V({\mathcal{O}}),\phi_{\mathcal{O}},\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\mathcal{O}})
. So the coefficient function
{\mathbf{c}}_{X}:{\mathbf{B}}/\tau\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
is defined by
{\mathcal{O}}\mapsto{\mathbf{c}}_{X}({\mathcal{O}})
.

Remark 4.9
.

Above in the case of
\epsilon=1
, i.e. the orthogonal case, we are in the situation where
G=O(V)
as in the article
[
5
]
of Derksen and Weyman. We will treat the case of
SO(V)
in the next section. Because we are in the orthogonal case, the coefficient function
{\mathbf{c}}_{X}:{\mathbf{B}}/\tau\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
is well-defined.

4.10.

We will now constructed an
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded isomorphism
T_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V
such that
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(u),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v)\rangle=\langle u,v\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}
for all
u,v\in V({\mathbf{c}})
. We will define this isomorphism on the bases
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathbf{c}}
of
V({\mathbf{c}})
and
{\mathcal{B}}
of
V
.

Let
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
. We will first define the function if
i>0
and secondly if
i=0
. In the following, let
c:{\mathbf{B}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
be the unique symmetric function corresponding to the coefficient function
{\mathbf{c}}:{\mathbf{B}}/\tau\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
.

Start with
i>0
. The proof is similar to the one when
|{\mathcal{I}}|
is even in proposition
3.10
. We get easily that

\{b\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid i\in Supp(b)\}\rightarrow\{b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid-i=\sigma(i)\in Supp(b^{\prime})\}\text{ defined by }b\mapsto\tau(b)

is a bijection. Fix a total order
b_{1}>b_{2}>\dots>b_{n}
on the set
\{b\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid i\in Supp(b)\}
such that
c(b_{r})\geq c(b_{s})
whenever
r<s
for
1\leq r,s\leq n
and let
b^{\prime}_{1}>b^{\prime}_{2}>\dots>b^{\prime}_{n}
be the total order on the set
\{b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid-i=\sigma(i)\in Supp(b^{\prime})\}
such that
b^{\prime}_{r}=\tau(b_{r})
for
1\leq r\leq n
.
Consider the Young diagram corresponding to the partition
c(b_{1})\geq c(b_{2})\geq\dots\geq c(b_{n})
. So the row indexed by
b_{r}
will have
c(b_{r})
columns. We would get the same Young diagram
c(b^{\prime}_{1})\geq c(b^{\prime}_{2})\geq\dots\geq c(b^{\prime}_{n})
if we had started with the total order
b^{\prime}_{1}>b^{\prime}_{2}>\dots>b^{\prime}_{n}
on the set
\{b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid-i=\sigma(i)\in Supp(b^{\prime})\}
. This follows from the fact that
c
is symmetric. This partition is a partition of
\delta_{i}
from
4.7
. In this Young diagram, we fill the boxes with the integers from 1 to
\delta_{i}
in strictly increasing order from left to right and top to bottom. We define
T_{{\mathbf{c}},i}:V_{i}({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V_{i}
(respectively
T_{{\mathbf{c}},-i}:V_{-i}({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V_{-i}
) to be the unique linear transformation such that if the entry in the box at row
r
and column
s
is
j
, then

T_{{\mathbf{c}},i}(v_{i}^{s}(b_{r}))=u_{i,j}
(respectively
T_{{\mathbf{c}},-i}(v_{-i}^{s}(b^{\prime}_{r}))=T_{{\mathbf{c}},-i}(v_{-i}^{s}(\tau(b_{r})))=u_{-i,j}
).

Secondly we consider the case
i=0
. In
2.15
(second case), we have noted that the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}\in{\mathbf{B}}/\tau
has overlapping
{\mathcal{I}}
-box supports if and only if
0\in Supp(b)
for any
{\mathcal{I}}
-box
b\in{\mathcal{O}}
.

In the symplectic case and when
|{\mathcal{I}}|
is odd, all the orbits
{\mathcal{O}}\in{\mathbf{B}}/\tau
are such that
{\mathcal{O}}=\{b,\tau(b)\}
with
\tau(b)\neq b
for some
b\in{\mathbf{B}}
, in other words,
|{\mathcal{O}}|=2
. From this observation, we get that
c(b)=c(\tau(b))={\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}})
. we have that

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\end{subarray}}c(b)=2\sum_{\mathcal{O}}{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}})=\delta_{0}=2\delta^{\prime}_{0}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\sum_{\mathcal{O}}{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}})=\delta^{\prime}_{0},

where the second sum over
{\mathcal{O}}
is over the set of orbits
{\mathcal{O}}
having overlapping
{\mathcal{I}}
-box supports. Fix a total order
{\mathcal{O}}_{1}>{\mathcal{O}}_{2}>\dots>{\mathcal{O}}_{n^{\prime}}
on the set

\{{\mathcal{O}}\in{\mathbf{B}}/\tau\mid{\mathcal{O}}\text{ has overlapping ${\mathcal{I}}$-box supports}\}

such that
{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}}_{r})\geq{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}}_{s})
whenever
r<s
for
1\leq r,s\leq n^{\prime}
. Consider the Young diagram corresponding to the partition
{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}}_{1})\geq{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}}_{2})\geq\dots\geq{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}}_{n^{\prime}})
. So the row indexed by
{\mathcal{O}}_{r}
will have
{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}}_{r})
columns. This partition is a partition of
\delta^{\prime}_{0}
from our observation above. In this Young diagram, we fill the boxes with the integers from 1 to
\delta^{\prime}_{0}
in strictly increasing order from left to right and top to bottom. We define
T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}:V_{0}({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V_{0}
to be the unique linear transformation such that if the entry in the box at row
r
corresponding to the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{r}
and column
s
, where
1\leq s\leq{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}}_{r})
is
j
, then we define

T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b))=\begin{cases}u_{0,j},&\text{if $b$ is above the principal diagonal;}\\
u_{0,-j},&\text{if $b$ is below the principal diagonal;}\\
u_{0,\epsilon^{k_{1}}j},&\text{if $b$ is on the principal diagonal and $b=b(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1})$;}\end{cases}

for
b\in{\mathcal{O}}_{r}
.

In the orthogonal case and when
|{\mathcal{I}}|
is odd, the
\langle\tau\rangle
-orbits are of two types: either the
\langle\tau\rangle
-orbit
{\mathcal{O}}=\{b\}
, where
b\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
\tau(b)=b
and
b
is on the principal diagonal, or the
\langle\tau\rangle
-orbit
{\mathcal{O}}=\{b,\tau(b)\}
, where
b\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
\tau(b)\neq b
and
b
is not on the principal diagonal. Denote by
{\mathbf{B}}^{+}
: the set of
{\mathcal{I}}
-boxes strictly above the principal diagonal and by
{\mathbf{B}}^{-}
: the set of
{\mathcal{I}}
-boxes strictly below the principal diagonal. Write

\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\\
\tau(b)=b\end{subarray}}c(b)=\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}\quad\text{ et }\quad\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}\\
0\in Supp(b)\end{subarray}}c(b)=\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}

Note that
\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}
has the same parity than
\delta_{0}
because

\delta_{0}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\end{subarray}}c(b)=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\\
\tau(b)=b\end{subarray}}c(b)+2\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}\\
0\in Supp(b)\end{subarray}}c(b)=\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+2\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}.

Here we have used the fact that the function
c:{\mathbf{B}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
is symmetric.

We get easily that
\{b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}\mid 0\in Supp(b)\}\rightarrow\{b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}\mid 0\in Supp(b)\}
defined by
b\mapsto\tau(b)
is a bijection. Fix a total order
b_{1}>b_{2}> \dots>b_{n}
on the set
\{b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}\mid 0\in Supp(b)\}
such that
c(b_{r})\geq c(b_{s})
whenever
r<s
for
1\leq r,s\leq n
and let
b^{\prime}_{1}>b^{\prime}_{2}>\dots>b^{\prime}_{n}
be the total order on the set
\{b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}\mid 0\in Supp(b^{\prime})\}
such that
b^{\prime}_{r}=\tau(b_{r})
for
1\leq r\leq n
. Consider the Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
corresponding to the partition
c(b_{1})\geq c(b_{2})\geq\dots\geq c(b_{n})
. So the row indexed by
b_{r}
will have
c(b_{r})
columns. We would get the same Young diagram
c(b^{\prime}_{1})\geq c(b^{\prime}_{2})\geq\dots\geq c(b^{\prime}_{n})
if we had started with the total order
b^{\prime}_{1}>b^{\prime}_{2}>\dots>b^{\prime}_{n}
, because
c
is symmetric. This partition is a partition of
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}
. In the Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
, fill the boxes with the integers from
1
to
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}
with strictly increasing order from left to right and top to bottom.

Consider also
\{b^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid 0\in Supp(b^{\prime\prime}),\tau(b^{\prime\prime})=b^{\prime\prime}\}
. Fix a total order
b^{\prime\prime}_{1}>b^{\prime\prime}_{2}>\dots>b^{\prime\prime}_{n^{\prime}}
on this last set such that
c(b^{\prime\prime}_{r})\geq c(b^{\prime\prime}_{s})
whenever
1\leq r<s\leq n^{\prime}
. Consider the Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
corresponding to the partition
c(b^{\prime\prime}_{1})\geq c(b^{\prime\prime}_{2})\geq\dots\geq c(b^{\prime\prime}_{n^{\prime}})
. So the row indexed by
b^{\prime\prime}_{r}
will have
c(b^{\prime\prime}_{r})
columns. This partition is a partition of
\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}
. In the Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
, fill the boxes with the integers from
(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)
to
(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})
with strictly increasing order from left to right and top to bottom.

To define
T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}:V_{0}({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V_{0}
, we need to consider two situations:
\delta_{0}
is either even or odd. Fix a square root
\sqrt{2}
of
2
and a square root
\sqrt{-2}
of
-2
in the field
{\mathbf{k}}
. Recall our hypothesis about the characteristic
p
of the field
{\mathbf{k}}
in
0.1
.

First we start with
\delta_{0}
being even and consequently
\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}
is even. Define with the same notation as above and for
b\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
0\in Supp(b)

T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b))=\begin{cases}u_{0,j},&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}$ and $b=b_{r}$;}\\
\\
u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j)},&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}$ and $b=\tau(b_{r})=b^{\prime}_{r}$;}\\
\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime})}\right),&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq j^{\prime}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}/2))$; }\\
\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime})}\right),&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}/2)+1)\leq j^{\prime}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})$; }\end{cases}

where the entry in the filled Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
in the box at row
r
and column
s
is
j
and the entry in the filled Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
in the box at row
r
and column
s
is
j^{\prime}
.

Secondly consider the case where
\delta_{0}
being odd and consequently
\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}
is also odd. Define with the same notation as above and for
b\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
0\in Supp(b)

T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b))=\begin{cases}u_{0,j},&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}$ and $b=b_{r}$;}\\
\\
u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j)},&\text{if $b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}$ and $b=\tau(b_{r})=b^{\prime}_{r}$;}\\
\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime})}\right),&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq j^{\prime}<(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+((\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+1)/2))$; }\\
\\
u_{0,j^{\prime}}&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$j^{\prime}=(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+1)/2)$; }\\
\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime})}\right),&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+1)/2)<j^{\prime}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})$; }\end{cases}

where the entry in the filled Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
in the box at row
r
and column
s
is
j
and the entry in the filled Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
in the box at row
r
and column
s
is
j^{\prime}
.

Finally
T_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V
is the direct sum of the linear transformations
T_{{\mathbf{c}},i}:V_{i}({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V_{i}
and
T_{{\mathbf{c}},-i}:V_{-i}({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V_{-i}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i>0
and of the linear transformation
T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}:V_{0}({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V_{0}
. Note that the dimension vector
\delta({\mathbf{c}})
of
{\mathbf{c}}
is
\delta({\mathbf{c}})=(\delta_{i})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}
, where
\delta_{i}=\dim(V_{i})
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
.

Lemma 4.11
.

With the above definition, we have that
T_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V
is an
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded isomorphism such that
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(u),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v)\rangle=\langle u,v\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}
for all
u,v\in V({\mathbf{c}})
.

Proof.

This proof is similar to the one in lemma
3.9
. There are just more cases to consider. To be complete , we have included the proof.

From our construction, it is clear that
T_{\mathbf{c}}
is an
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded isomorphism.
To check that
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(u),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v)\rangle=\langle u,v\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}
for all
u,v\in V({\mathbf{c}})
, it is enough to check it on the basis
{\mathcal{B}}_{\mathbf{c}}
. We want to consider
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}))\rangle
where
i,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i\in Supp(b)
,
1\leq s\leq c(b)
,
i^{\prime}\in Supp(b^{\prime})
and
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
. Because
T_{\mathbf{c}}
is
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded, our hypothesis in
4.2
on the basis
{\mathcal{B}}
of
V
and our construction in
4.7
, we get if
i+i^{\prime}\neq 0
, that

\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}))\rangle=0=\langle v_{i}^{s}(b),v_{i^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}.

Now let
i^{\prime}=-i
and assume first that
i>0
. Consider the same total orders as above on the sets
\{b\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid i\in Supp(b)\}
and
\{b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid-i=\sigma(i)\in Supp(b)\}
. Proceeding as above with the Young diagram, assume that
b=b_{r}
in the total order
b_{1}>b_{2}>\dots>b_{n}
on the set
\{b\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid i\in Supp(b)\}
and
b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}
in the total order
b^{\prime}_{1}>b^{\prime}_{2}>\dots>b^{\prime}_{n}
on the set
\{b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid-i=\sigma(b)\in Supp(b^{\prime})\}
, the entry in the box at row
r
and column
s
is
j
and the entry in the box at row
r^{\prime}
and column
s^{\prime}
is
j^{\prime}
, then

\displaystyle\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}))\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{s}(b_{r})),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}}))\rangle=\langle u_{i,j},u_{-i,j^{\prime}}\rangle

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $j=j^{\prime}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise;}\end{cases}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $r=r^{\prime}$ and $s=s^{\prime}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

If we now consider the bilinear form on
V({\mathbf{c}})
, we have

\displaystyle\langle v_{i}^{s}(b),v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}

\displaystyle=\langle v_{i}^{s}(b_{r}),v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}_{r^{\prime}})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=\langle v_{i}^{s}(b_{r}),v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(\tau(b_{r^{\prime}}))\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $r=r^{\prime}$ and $s=s^{\prime}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

So if
i>0
, then
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}))\rangle=\langle v_{i}^{s}(b),v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}
.

If
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i<0
, then

\displaystyle\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}))\rangle

\displaystyle=\epsilon\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{s}(b))\rangle

\displaystyle=\epsilon\langle v_{-i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),v_{i}^{s}(b)\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=\langle v_{-i}^{s}(b),v_{i}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}.

Assume now that
i=0
and we are in the symplectic case. Consider the same total order as above on the set

\{{\mathcal{O}}\in{\mathbf{B}}/\tau\mid{\mathcal{O}}\text{ has overlapping ${\mathcal{I}}$-box supports}\}.

Proceeding as above with the Young diagram, assume that
0\in Supp(b)\cap Supp(b^{\prime})
,
b\in{\mathcal{O}}_{r}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{r^{\prime}}
, the entry in the box at row
r
and column
s
is
j
and the entry in the box at row
r^{\prime}
and column
s^{\prime}
is
j^{\prime}
. From our definition, we have that

T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s}(b))=\begin{cases}u_{0,j},&\text{if $b$ is above the principal diagonal;}\\
u_{0,-j},&\text{if $b$ is below the principal diagonal;}\\
u_{0,\epsilon^{k_{1}}j},&\text{if $b$ is on the principal diagonal and $b=b(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1})$;}\end{cases}

and

T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}))=\begin{cases}u_{0,j^{\prime}},&\text{if $b^{\prime}$ is above the principal diagonal;}\\
u_{0,-j^{\prime}},&\text{if $b^{\prime}$ is below the principal diagonal;}\\
u_{0,\epsilon^{k^{\prime}_{1}}j^{\prime}},&\text{if $b^{\prime}$ is on the principal diagonal and $b^{\prime}=b(i^{\prime}_{1},j^{\prime}_{1},k^{\prime}_{1})$.}\end{cases}

If
s^{\prime}\neq s
, then
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}))\rangle=0
, because
j\neq\pm j^{\prime}
from our construction above.

If
s^{\prime}=s
and
b^{\prime}\not\in{\mathcal{O}}_{r}
, then
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s}(b^{\prime}))\rangle=0
, because
r^{\prime}\neq r
and again
j\neq\pm j^{\prime}
.

If
s^{\prime}=s
and
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{r}
, then either
b^{\prime}=b
or
b^{\prime}=\tau(b)
. Recall that in the symplectic case, we never have
\tau(b)=b
, in other words the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{r}=\{b,\tau(b)\}
has cardinality 2. Now in the first case:
b^{\prime}=b
, we get that

\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s}(b))\rangle=0
because
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
is a symplectic bilinear form.
In the second case,
b^{\prime}=\tau(b)
, we get that
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{0}^{s}(\tau(b)))\rangle
is equal to

\begin{cases}\langle u_{0,j},u_{0,-j}\rangle=1&\text{if $b$ is above the principal diagonal;}\\
\langle u_{0,-j},u_{0,j}\rangle=\epsilon&\text{if $b$ is below the principal diagonal;}\\
\langle u_{0,\epsilon^{k_{1}}j},u_{0,\epsilon^{(1-k_{1})}j}\rangle=\epsilon^{k_{1}},&\text{if $b=b(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1})$ is on the principal diagonal.}\end{cases}

In this last case when the
{\mathcal{I}}
-box
b
is on the principal diagonal, there are two situations: either
b=b(i_{1},j_{1},0)
or
b=b(i_{1},j_{1},1)
. We have respectively
\tau(b)=b(i_{1},j_{1},1)
and
\tau(b)=b(i_{1},j_{1},0)
. So we get

\begin{cases}\langle u_{0,j},u_{0,-j}\rangle=1,&\text{if $b=b(i_{1},j_{1},0)$ is on the principal diagonal.}\\
\langle u_{0,-j},u_{0,j}\rangle=\epsilon,&\text{if $b=b(i_{1},j_{1},1)$ is on the principal diagonal.}\end{cases}

Note that we have

\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $s^{\prime}=s$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$ and $b$ is above the principal diagonal;}\\
\epsilon,&\text{if $s^{\prime}=s$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$ and $b$ is below the principal diagonal;}\\
\epsilon^{k_{1}},&\text{if $s^{\prime}=s$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$ and $b=b(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1})$ is on the}\\
&\text{principal diagonal; }\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

So we have
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{s}(b)),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}))\rangle=\langle v_{i}^{s}(b),v_{i^{\prime}}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}
in the symplectic case, where
i,i^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{I}}
,
i\in Supp(b)
,
1\leq s\leq c(b)
,
i^{\prime}\in Supp(b^{\prime})
and
1\leq s^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
.

Assume now that
i=0
and we are in the orthogonal case. Consider the same total order:
b_{1}>b_{2}>\dots>b_{n}
on the set
\{b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}\mid 0\in Supp(b)\}
as above, the corresponding total order:
b^{\prime}_{1}>b^{\prime}_{2}>\dots>b^{\prime}_{n}
on the set
\{b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}\mid 0\in Supp(b^{\prime})\}
where
b^{\prime}_{r}=\tau(b_{r})
for
1\leq r\leq n
as above and the same total order:
b^{\prime\prime}_{1}>b^{\prime\prime}_{2}>\dots>b^{\prime\prime}_{n^{\prime}}
on the set
\{b^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid 0\in Supp(b^{\prime\prime}),\tau(b^{\prime\prime})=b^{\prime\prime}\}
as above. By our definition, we have

\begin{cases}T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b))=u_{0,j},&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}$ and $b=b_{r}$;}\\
\\
T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b^{\prime}))=u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j)},&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}$ and $b^{\prime}=b^{\prime}_{r}=\tau(b_{r})$;}\end{cases}

where
j
is the entry in the Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
in the box at row
r
and column
s
. In this case, we have that
1\leq j\leq\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}
and
(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq(\delta_{0}+1-j)\leq\delta_{0}
.

We also have that

T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b))=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime})}\right),&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq j^{\prime}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}/2))$; }\\
\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime})}\right),&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}/2)+1)\leq j^{\prime}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})$; }\end{cases}

when
\delta_{0}
is even and

T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b))=\begin{cases}\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime})}\right),&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq j^{\prime}<(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+((\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+1)/2))$; }\\
\\
u_{0,j^{\prime}}&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$j^{\prime}=(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+1)/2)$; }\\
\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime})}\right),&\text{if $b\in{\mathbf{B}}$, $\tau(b)=b$, $b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}$ and }\\
&\text{$(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+1)/2)<j^{\prime}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})$; }\end{cases}

when
\delta_{0}
is odd, where the entry in the filled Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
in the box at row
r
and column
s
is
j^{\prime}
.
In this case we have that
(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq j^{\prime}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})
and
(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime})\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})
. Because of the above inequalities for
j
and
j^{\prime}
and our hypothesis in
4.2
, we have for
b,{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
0\in Supp(b)\cap Supp({\overline{b}})
,
1\leq s\leq c(b)
and
1\leq{\overline{s}}\leq c({\overline{b}})
that

\langle T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}}))\rangle\neq 0
only in the following cases:

•

b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}
,
{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}
;

•

b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}
,
{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}
;

•

b,{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
\tau(b)=b
,
\tau({\overline{b}})=({\overline{b}})
;

and similarly we have that

\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}\neq 0
only in the same cases as above:

•

b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}
,
{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}
;

•

b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}
,
{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}
;

•

b,{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
\tau(b)=b
,
\tau({\overline{b}})=({\overline{b}})
.

We will now consider each of these cases.

If
b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}
,
0\in Supp(b)
,
b=b_{r}
,
1\leq s\leq c(b_{r})
,
{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}
,
0\in Supp({\overline{b}})
,
{\overline{b}}=b^{\prime}_{\overline{r}}=\tau(b_{\overline{r}})
,
1\leq{\overline{s}}\leq c({\overline{b}})
and
j
(respectively
{\overline{j}}
) is the entry in the Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{1}
in the box at row
r
(respectively
{\overline{r}}
) and column
s
(respectively
{\overline{s}}
), then

\displaystyle\langle T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}}))\rangle

\displaystyle=\langle u_{0,j},u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-{\overline{j}})}\rangle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $j={\overline{j}}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

\displaystyle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $r={\overline{r}}$ and $s={\overline{s}}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

but we also have

\langle v_{0}^{s}(b_{r}),v_{0}^{\overline{s}}(b^{\prime}_{\overline{r}})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=\langle v_{0}^{s}(b_{r}),v_{0}^{\overline{s}}(\tau(b_{\overline{r}}))\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $r={\overline{r}}$ and $s={\overline{s}}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and consequently

\langle T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}}))\rangle=\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}.

If
b\in{\mathbf{B}}^{-}
,
0\in Supp(b)
,
b=b^{\prime}_{r}=\tau(b_{r})
,
1\leq s\leq c(b^{\prime}_{r})
,
{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}^{+}
,
0\in Supp({\overline{b}})
,
{\overline{b}}=b_{\overline{r}}
,
1\leq{\overline{s}}\leq c({\overline{b}})
, then we just use the previous case, as well as the symmetry of the bilinear forms
\langle\ ,\ \rangle
and
\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\mathbf{c}}
to get that

\langle T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}}))\rangle=\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}.

We have now to consider the last case where
b,{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
0\in Supp(b)\cap Supp({\overline{b}})
,
\tau(b)=b
and
\tau({\overline{b}})={\overline{b}}
. First note that if
\delta_{0}
is even,
(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq j^{\prime}_{1},j^{\prime}_{2}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}/2))
and
(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}/2)+1)\leq j^{\prime\prime}_{1},j^{\prime\prime}_{2}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})
,we get easily that

\left\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}_{1}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime}_{1})}\right),\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}_{2}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime}_{2})}\right)\right\rangle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $j^{\prime}_{1}=j^{\prime}_{2}$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise;}\end{cases}

\left\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}_{1}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime}_{1})}\right),\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime\prime}_{1}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime\prime}_{1})}\right)\right\rangle=0

for all
j^{\prime}_{1}
and
j^{\prime\prime}_{1}
as above;

\left\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime\prime}_{1}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime\prime}_{1})}\right),\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime\prime}_{2}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime\prime}_{2})}\right)\right\rangle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $j^{\prime\prime}_{1}=j^{\prime\prime}_{2}$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise;}\end{cases}

while if
\delta_{0}
is odd,
(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq j^{\prime}_{1},j^{\prime}_{2}<(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+1)/2)
,
(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+1)/2))<j^{\prime\prime}_{1},j^{\prime\prime}_{2}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})
and
j^{\prime\prime\prime}=(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+(\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}+1)/2)
, we also get easily that

\left\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}_{1}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime}_{1})}\right),\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}_{2}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime}_{2})}\right)\right\rangle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $j^{\prime}_{1}=j^{\prime}_{2}$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise;}\end{cases}

\left\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}_{1}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime}_{1})}\right),\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime\prime}_{1}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime\prime}_{1})}\right)\right\rangle=0

for all
j^{\prime}_{1}
and
j^{\prime\prime}_{1}
as above;

\left\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime\prime}_{1}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime\prime}_{1})}\right),\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime\prime}_{2}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime\prime}_{2})}\right)\right\rangle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $j^{\prime\prime}_{1}=j^{\prime\prime}_{2}$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise;}\end{cases}

\left\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime}_{1}}+u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime}_{1})}\right),u_{0,j^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right\rangle=\left\langle\frac{1}{\sqrt{-2}}\left(u_{0,j^{\prime\prime}_{1}}-u_{0,(\delta_{0}+1-j^{\prime\prime}_{1})}\right),u_{0,j^{\prime\prime\prime}}\right\rangle=0

and
\langle u_{0,j^{\prime\prime\prime}},u_{0,j^{\prime\prime\prime}}\rangle=1
.

Consider now
b,{\overline{b}}\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
0\in Supp(b)\cap Supp({\overline{b}})
,
\tau(b)=b
,
\tau({\overline{b}})={\overline{b}}
,
b=b^{\prime\prime}_{r}
,
{\overline{b}}=b^{\prime\prime}_{\overline{r}}
and let
j
(respectively
{\overline{j}}
) be the entry in the Young diagram
{\mathcal{Y}}_{2}
in the box at row
r
(respectively
{\overline{r}}
) and column
s
(respectively
{\overline{s}}
). In this case,
(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+1)\leq j,{\overline{j}}\leq(\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}+\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0})
. By our above computations, we get that

\langle T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}}))\rangle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $j={\overline{j}}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $r={\overline{r}}$ and $s={\overline{s}}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

but we also have

\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{\overline{s}}(\overline{b})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=\langle v_{0}^{s}(b^{\prime\prime}_{r}),v_{0}^{\overline{s}}(b^{\prime\prime}_{\overline{r}})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $r={\overline{r}}$ and $s={\overline{s}}$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and consequently

\langle T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{s}(b)),T_{{\mathbf{c}},0}(v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}}))\rangle=\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{\overline{s}}({\overline{b}})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}.

Thus we have proved that
\langle T_{\mathbf{c}}(u),T_{\mathbf{c}}(v)\rangle=\langle u,v\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}
for all
u,v\in V({\mathbf{c}})
.

∎

Proposition 4.12
.

Let
\delta=(\delta_{i})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}
be as in
4.2
and
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}/G^{\iota}
denotes the set of
G^{\iota}
-orbits in
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
. Then the map

\Upsilon:{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}/G^{\iota}\rightarrow{\mathfrak{C}}_{\delta}\quad\text{ defined by }\quad G^{\iota}\cdot X\mapsto{\mathbf{c}}_{X},

is a well-defined bijection.

Proof.

By our last observation in
4.8
, if
G^{\iota}\cdot X=G^{\iota}\cdot X^{\prime}
, then the corresponding
\epsilon
-representations for
X
and
X^{\prime}
are isomorphic, we get that
{\mathbf{c}}_{X}={\mathbf{c}}_{X^{\prime}}
and the function
\Upsilon
is well-defined.

To show that
\Upsilon
is an injective function, assume that
{\mathbf{c}}_{X}={\mathbf{c}}_{X^{\prime}}
, then this means that, by the Krull-Remak-Schmidt theorem, the
\epsilon
-representations for
X
and
X^{\prime}
are isomorphic. So
X
and
X^{\prime}
are in the same
G^{\iota}
-orbit and
G^{\iota}\cdot X=G^{\iota}\cdot X^{\prime}
.

To prove that
\Upsilon
is surjective, we need to show that there is an element
X\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
such that
{\mathbf{c}}_{X}={\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{\delta}
. Consider
X=T_{\mathbf{c}}E_{\mathbf{c}}T_{\mathbf{c}}^{-1}:V\rightarrow V
, where
T_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V
is the
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded isomorphism defined in
4.10
. Because
E_{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}(V({\mathbf{c}}))
and lemma
4.11
,
X\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
. From our construction of
E_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V({\mathbf{c}})
, it is isomorphic as
{\mathcal{I}}
-graded and
\epsilon
-representation to the direct sum of
{\mathbf{c}}({\mathcal{O}})
copies of the linear transformation
E_{\mathcal{O}}:V({\mathcal{O}})\rightarrow V({\mathcal{O}})
. By carrying this direct sum using
T_{\mathbf{c}}
to
V
, we do get that
{\mathbf{c}}_{X}={\mathbf{c}}
and the proof is complete.

∎

Notation 4.13
.

Let
\delta=(\delta_{i})_{i\in{\mathcal{I}}}
be such that
\delta_{i}=\dim(V_{i})
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
. If
{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{\delta}
, we will denote by
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}
: the unique
G^{\iota}
-orbit in
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
such that
\Upsilon({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}})={\mathbf{c}}
.

Remark 4.14
.

Using the isomorphism
T_{\mathbf{c}}:V({\mathbf{c}})\rightarrow V
, we can transfer results about the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}
in the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}
to the orbit of
E_{\mathbf{c}}
in the Lie algebra

{\mathfrak{g}}(V({\mathbf{c}}))
.

4.15.

To compute the dimension of the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}
corresponding to
{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{\delta}
, we will proceed as in the previous section by considering a parabolic subalgebra
{\mathfrak{p}}_{X}
associated by Lusztig to an element
X\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}\subseteq{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
and then using the formula

\dim({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}})=\dim({\mathfrak{g}}_{0})-\dim({\mathfrak{p}}_{0})+\dim({\mathfrak{p}}_{2}),

where
{\mathfrak{p}}
is the parabolic subalgebra
{\mathfrak{p}}_{X}
.

We now want to use this formula to compute the dimension
\dim({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}})
of the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}
in term of the coefficient function
{\mathbf{c}}
. This is done in the next proposition.

Note that we have to be careful in the orthogonal case, because the above formula for the dimension
\dim({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}})
of the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}
proved using 5.4 (a) and 5.9 of
[
9
]
is under the hypothesis that the group
G
is connected. In the orthogonal case,
G
is the orthogonal group
O(V)
and
G^{\iota}
is not connected as we saw in lemma
4.3
(a). From our definition of
\iota
, it is easy to see that the connected component of the identity
(G^{\iota})^{0}
of
G^{\iota}
is equal to
(G^{0})^{\iota}
: the centralizer of
\iota
in the connected component
G^{0}
of
G
. Here
G^{0}
is the special orthogonal group and is connected. Because of this observation, we can also used the above formula for the dimension of the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}
.

Proposition 4.16
.

Let
{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathfrak{C}}_{\delta}
and consider the symmetric function
c:{\mathbf{B}}\rightarrow{\mathbb{N}}
corresponding to
{\mathbf{c}}
. Then the dimension
\dim({\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}})
of the orbit
{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}
is equal to

\left[\begin{aligned} &\frac{\delta_{0}(\delta_{0}-\epsilon)}{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in{\mathcal{I}}\\
0<i\end{subarray}}\delta_{i}^{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in{\mathcal{I}}\\
0\leq i<(2m-2)\\
(b,b^{\prime})\in{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathbf{B}}\\
i\in Supp(b)\\
(i+2)\in Supp(b^{\prime})\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})\end{subarray}}c(b)c(b^{\prime})-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in{\mathcal{I}}\\
i>0\\
(b,b^{\prime})\in{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathbf{B}}\\
i\in Supp(b)\cap Supp(b^{\prime})\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})\end{subarray}}c(b)c(b^{\prime})\\
\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(b,b^{\prime})\in{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\cap Supp(b^{\prime})\\
b\neq\tau(b^{\prime})\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})\end{subarray}}c(b)c(b^{\prime})-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(\tau(b))\end{subarray}}c(b)(c(b)-\epsilon)\end{aligned}\right]

Proof.

Taking into account our observation in
4.15
in the orthogonal case and our remark
4.14
, we will assume that
V=V({\mathbf{c}})
and
{\mathfrak{g}}
will be
{\mathfrak{g}}(V({\mathbf{c}}))
. We have that
\dim(V_{i})=\delta_{i}
for all
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
from the fact that
\delta({\mathbf{c}})=\delta
.
We have constructed in
4.7
a Jacobson-Morozov triple
(E_{\mathbf{c}},H_{\mathbf{c}},F_{\mathbf{c}})
with
E_{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}
; in other words we have

[H_{\mathbf{c}},E_{\mathbf{c}}]=2E_{\mathbf{c}},\qquad[H_{\mathbf{c}},F_{\mathbf{c}}]=-2F_{\mathbf{c}},\qquad[E_{\mathbf{c}},F_{\mathbf{c}}]=H_{\mathbf{c}},

where
E_{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
,
H_{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
and
F_{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathfrak{g}}_{-2}
and such that
E_{\mathbf{c}}\in{\mathcal{O}}_{\mathbf{c}}
. Thus we have a Lie algebra homomorphism
\phi:{\mathfrak{s}l}_{2}({\mathbf{k}})\rightarrow{\mathfrak{g}}
such that

\phi\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}=E_{\mathbf{c}},\qquad\phi\begin{pmatrix}1&\phantom{-}0\\
0&-1\end{pmatrix}=H_{\mathbf{c}},\qquad\phi\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}=F_{\mathbf{c}}.

As in definition
3.14
, let
\widetilde{\phi}:SL_{2}({\mathbf{k}})\rightarrow G
be the homomorphism of algebraic groups whose differential is
\phi
and by
\iota^{\prime}:{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}\rightarrow G
: the homomorphism defined by

\iota^{\prime}(t)=\widetilde{\phi}\begin{pmatrix}t&0\phantom{-}\\
0&t^{-1}\end{pmatrix}\qquad\text{ for all $t\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}$.}

We have the grading for the Lie algebra
{\mathfrak{g}}=\oplus_{r}({}_{r}{\mathfrak{g}})
where

({}_{r}{\mathfrak{g}})=\{X\in{\mathfrak{g}}\mid Ad(\iota^{\prime}(t))(X)=t^{r}X\quad\text{ for all $t\in{\mathbf{k}}^{\times}$}\}

By taking the derivative, we get that

({}_{r}{\mathfrak{g}})=\{X\in{\mathfrak{g}}\mid ad(H_{\mathbf{c}})(X)=rX\}.

By lemma
4.3
(c), we have that

\dim({\mathfrak{g}}_{0})=\frac{\delta_{0}(\delta_{0}-\epsilon)}{2}+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in{\mathcal{I}}\\
i>0\end{subarray}}\delta_{i}^{2}.

Let
{\mathfrak{p}}
be the parabolic subalgebra associated to the nilpotent element
E_{\mathbf{c}}
. From the definition of
{\mathfrak{p}}
, we get that
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}
and
{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}
are such that

{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}=\bigoplus_{r\geq 0}({}_{r}{\mathfrak{g}}_{0})\qquad\text{ and }\qquad{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}=\bigoplus_{r\geq 2}({}_{r}{\mathfrak{g}}_{2})

By our description of
{\mathfrak{g}}_{0}
and
{\mathfrak{g}}_{2}
in lemma
4.3
, we get that
{\mathfrak{p}}_{0}={\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}_{0}\oplus{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime\prime}_{0}
, where

{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}_{0}=\bigoplus_{r\geq 0}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in{\mathcal{I}}\\
0<i\end{subarray}}\{(Z_{i}:V_{i}\rightarrow V_{i})\in Hom(V_{i},V_{i})\mid ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{i}})Z_{i}=rZ_{i}\},

{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime\prime}_{0}=\bigoplus_{r\geq 0}\left\{Z_{0}:V_{0}\rightarrow V_{0}\in Hom(V_{0},V_{0})\left|\ \begin{aligned} &ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})Z_{0}=rZ_{0}\text{ and }\\
&\langle Z_{0}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{0}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0\\
&\text{ for all $v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}$.}\end{aligned}\right.\right\},

and

{\mathfrak{p}}_{2}=\bigoplus_{r\geq 2}\bigoplus_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in{\mathcal{I}}\\
0\leq i<(2m-2)\end{subarray}}\{(X_{i}:V_{i}\rightarrow V_{i+2})\in Hom(V_{i},V_{i+2})\mid ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{i}})X_{i}=rX_{i}\}.

We can give bases for the spaces
Hom(V_{i},V_{i})
and
Hom(V_{i},V_{i+2})
that are eigenvectors for
ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{i}})
. From our construction of the triple
\{E_{\mathbf{c}},H_{\mathbf{c}},F_{\mathbf{c}}\}
, we have for each
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
a basis

{\mathcal{B}}_{i}=\{v_{i}^{j}(b)\in V_{i}\mid b\in{\mathbf{B}},i\in Supp(b),1\leq j\leq c(b)\}

of
V_{i}
.

Let now
i\in{\mathcal{I}}
and
0\leq i<(2m-2)
. For each
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}
with
i\in Supp(b)
,
(i+2)\in Supp(b^{\prime})
,
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
, let
X_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}\in Hom(V_{i},V_{i+2})
be defined on the basis
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
by

X_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{i}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}v_{i+2}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{if $b^{\prime\prime}=b$ and $j^{\prime\prime}=j$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Clearly these linear transformations
X_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
with
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
i\in Supp(b)
,
(i+2)\in Supp(b^{\prime})
,
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
form a basis of
Hom(V_{i},V_{i+2})
.

We have that
H_{\mathbf{c}}(v_{i}^{j}(b))=h_{i}(b)v_{i}^{j}(b)
for all
b\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
i\in Supp(b)
and
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and
X_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
is an eigenvector of
ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{i}})
with

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{i}})(X_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})})=(h_{(i+2)}(b^{\prime})-h_{i}(b))X_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}

by computing both sides on the basis vector
v_{i}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
. Consequently we get that

\dim({\mathfrak{p}}_{2})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in{\mathcal{I}}\\
0\leq i< (2m-2)\\
(b,b^{\prime})\in{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathbf{B}}\\
i\in Supp(b)\\
(i+2)\in Supp(b^{\prime})\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})\end{subarray}}c(b)c(b^{\prime})

because
(h_{i+2}(b^{\prime})-h_{i}(b))\geq 2
is equivalent to
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})
as we saw in the proof of proposition
3.16

We can now consider the vector space
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}_{0}
. Let
i>0
. For each
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}
with
i\in Supp(b)\cap Supp(b^{\prime})
,
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
, let
Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}\in Hom(V_{i},V_{i})
be defined on the basis
{\mathcal{B}}_{i}
by

Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{i}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}v_{i}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{if $b^{\prime\prime}=b$ and $j^{\prime\prime}=j$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

These linear transformations
Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
with
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathbf{B}}
,
i\in Supp(b)\cap Supp(b^{\prime})
,
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
form a basis of
Hom(V_{i},V_{i})
. Moreover
Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
is an eigenvector of
ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{i}})
with

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}})(Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})})=(h_{i}(b^{\prime})-h_{i}(b))Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}

by computing both sides on the basis vector
v_{i}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
. Consequently

\dim({\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime}_{0})=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in{\mathcal{I}}\\
i>0\\
(b,b^{\prime})\in{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathbf{B}}\\
i\in Supp(b)\cap Supp(b^{\prime})\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})\end{subarray}}c(b)c(b^{\prime})

because
(h_{i}(b^{\prime})-h_{i}(b))\geq 0
is equivalent to
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})
as we saw in the proof of proposition
3.16

We must now consider the dimension
\dim({\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime\prime}_{0})
and we will prove that

\dim({\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime\prime}_{0})=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(b,b^{\prime})\in{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\cap Supp(b^{\prime})\\
b\neq\tau(b^{\prime})\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})\end{subarray}}c(b)c(b^{\prime})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(\tau(b))\end{subarray}}c(b)(c(b)-\epsilon)\right).

Recall that
V_{0}
has a basis
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}=\{v_{0}^{j}(b)\mid b\in{\mathbf{B}},0\in Supp(b),1\leq j\leq c(b)\}
such that

\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{s^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})\rangle=\begin{cases}1,&\text{if $s^{\prime}=s$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$ and $b$ is above the principal diagonal;}\\
\epsilon,&\text{if $s^{\prime}=s$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$ and $b$ is below the principal diagonal;}\\
\epsilon^{k_{1}},&\text{if $s^{\prime}=s$, $b^{\prime}=\tau(b)$ and $b=b(i_{1},j_{1},k_{1})$ is on the}\\
&\text{principal diagonal; }\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

Let
{\mathcal{E}}=\{b\in{\mathbf{B}}\mid 0\in Supp(b)\}
and
(Z_{0}:V_{0}\rightarrow V_{0})\in Hom(V_{0},V_{0})
be such that

(2)

\langle Z_{0}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{0}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0\quad\text{for all $v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}$.}

For
v_{0}^{j}(b),v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})\in{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
, then we can write

Z_{0}(v_{0}^{j}(b))=\sum_{(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})}\xi_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})}v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(\tau(b^{\prime\prime}))

where the sum runs over all
(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})
such that
b^{\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}
and
1\leq j^{\prime\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime\prime})
and

Z_{0}(v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}))=\sum_{(b^{\prime\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime\prime})}\xi_{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}^{(b^{\prime\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime\prime})}v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime\prime}}(\tau(b^{\prime\prime\prime}))

where the sum runs over all
(b^{\prime\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime\prime})
such that
b^{\prime\prime\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}
and
1\leq j^{\prime\prime\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime\prime\prime})
,

We will first consider the symplectic case.
The set
{\mathcal{E}}
can be partitionned as follows:
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{E}}^{+}\coprod{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}^{+}

\displaystyle=\{b\in{\mathcal{E}}\mid b(i,j,0)\text{ where $i,j\in{\mathcal{I}}$, $i\geq j$ and $i+j\geq 0$}\}\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}^{-}

\displaystyle=\{b\in{\mathcal{E}}\mid b(i,j,0)\text{ where $i,j\in{\mathcal{I}}$, $i\geq j$ and $i+j<0$}\}

\displaystyle\bigcup\{b\in{\mathcal{E}}\mid b(i,-i,1)\text{ where $i\in{\mathcal{I}}$ and $i\geq 0$}\}.

{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
consists of the
{\mathcal{I}}
-boxes strictly above the principal diagonale and of half of the
{\mathcal{I}}
-boxes on the principal diagonale, while
{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
consists of the
{\mathcal{I}}
-boxes strictly below the principal diagonale and the other half of the
{\mathcal{I}}
-boxes on the principal diagonale. Note that if
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
, then
\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{s}(\tau(b))\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=1
for any
1\leq s\leq c(b)
, while if
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
, then
\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{s}(\tau(b))\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=-1
for any
1\leq s\leq c(b)
.

Setting
v=v_{0}^{j}(b)
and
v^{\prime}=v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime})
in the equation (
2
) and using the values of
\langle\ ,\ \rangle_{\mathbf{c}}
in notation
4.7
, we get the equations:

(3)

\left\{\begin{aligned} -\xi_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}+\xi_{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}^{(b,j)}&=0,\quad\text{ if $b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}$;}\\
\\
\xi_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}+\xi_{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}^{(b,j)}&=0,\quad\text{ if $b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}$ and $b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}$;}\\
\\
-\xi_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}-\xi_{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}^{(b,j)}&=0,\quad\text{ if $b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}$ and $b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}$;}\\
\\
\xi_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}-\xi_{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}^{(b,j)}&=0,\quad\text{ if $b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}$;}\end{aligned}\right.

Reciprocally if the above equations are satisfied for all
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}
,
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
, then
Z_{0}
defined above on the vectors
v_{0}^{j}(b)
will be such that the equation (
2
) is satisfied.

We will now give a basis of the vector space

\{(Z_{0}:V_{0}\rightarrow V_{0})\in Hom(V_{0},V_{0})\mid\langle Z_{0}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{0}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0\text{ for all $v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}$}\}

and each of its element will be an eigenvector of
ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})
.

Order the basis
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
such that the first elements are the basis vectors of the form
v_{0}^{j}(b)
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
and
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and the following elements are the basis vectors of the form
v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b))
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
and
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and if
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
, then
v_{0}^{j}(b)
and
v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b))
are respectively in the
m_{1}^{th}
and
m_{2}^{th}
positions with
(m_{2}-m_{1})=\delta_{0}^{\prime}
. Relative to this ordered basis, the linear transformation
Z_{0}
as above has the form

(4)

\begin{pmatrix}A_{11}&A_{12}\\
A_{21}&A_{22}\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{with}\quad A_{22}=-A^{t}_{11},\quad A_{12}=A^{t}_{12},\quad A_{21}=A^{t}_{21}

We will define linear transformations
Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}:V_{0}\rightarrow V_{0}
when
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}
,
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
for three cases:

(i)

either
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
or
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
;

(ii)

(b,j)\neq(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})
and either
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
or
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
;

(iii)

(b,j)=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})
and either
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
or
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
.

Case (i): If either
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
or
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
, we define

Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(b,j)$;}\\
-v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b)),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

This means

\xi_{(b_{1},j_{1})}^{(b_{2},j_{2})}=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(b,j)$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
-1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(b,j)$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We get easily that

\langle Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0

for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
from the equations (
3
). Note that the pair
\tau(b^{\prime}),\tau(b)
falls also in the case (i) and it is easy to see that
Z_{(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})}^{(\tau(b),j)}=-Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
by evaluating both sides on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
.

By looking on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}=(h_{0}(b^{\prime})-h_{0}(b))Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}.

We have used lemma
2.14
(a) in the proof of this formula.

Case (ii): If
(b,j)\neq(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})
and either
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
or
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
, we define

Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(b,j)$;}\\
v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b)),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

This means

\xi_{(b_{1},j_{1})}^{(b_{2},j_{2})}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(b,j)$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(b,j)$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We get easily that

\langle Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0

for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
from the equations (
3
). Note that the pair
\tau(b^{\prime}),\tau(b)
falls also in the case (ii) and it is easy to see that
Z_{(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})}^{(\tau(b),j)}=Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
by evaluating both sides on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
.

By looking on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}=(h_{0}(b^{\prime})-h_{0}(b))Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}.

We have used lemma
2.14
(a) in the proof of this formula.

Case (iii): If
(b,j)=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})
and either
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
or
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{-}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{+}
, we define

Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(b,j)$;}\\
0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

This means

\xi_{(b_{1},j_{1})}^{(b_{2},j_{2})}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(b,j)$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(\tau(b),j)$;}\\
0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We get easily that

\langle Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0

for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
from the equations (
3
).

By looking on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}=(h_{0}(b^{\prime})-h_{0}(b))Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}.

We have used lemma
2.14
(a) in the proof of this formula.

From this, we get in the symplectic case that

\dim({\mathfrak{p}}^{\prime\prime}_{0})=\frac{1}{2}\left(\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(b,b^{\prime})\in{\mathbf{B}}\times{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\cap Supp(b^{\prime})\\
b\neq\tau(b^{\prime})\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})\end{subarray}}c(b)c(b^{\prime})+\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}b\in{\mathbf{B}}\\
0\in Supp(b)\\
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(\tau(b))\end{subarray}}c(b)(c(b)+1)\right).

Note that we have also used the fact that
(h_{0}(b^{\prime})-h_{0}(b))\geq 0
is equivalent to
(0-h_{0}(b))\geq(0-h_{0}(b^{\prime}))
; in other words,
\lambda(b)\geq\lambda(b^{\prime})
.

We must now consider the orthogonal case. The set
{\mathcal{E}}
can be partitionned as
{\mathcal{E}}={\mathcal{E}}^{>0}\coprod{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}\coprod{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
, where

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}

\displaystyle=\{b\in{\mathcal{E}}\mid b(i,j,0)\text{ where $i,j\in{\mathcal{I}}$, $i\geq j$ and $(i+j)>0$}\}

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}

\displaystyle=\{b\in{\mathcal{E}}\mid b(i,j,0)\text{ where $i,j\in{\mathcal{I}}$, $i\geq j$ and $(i+j)=0$}\}\quad\text{ and }

\displaystyle{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}

\displaystyle=\{b\in{\mathcal{E}}\mid b(i,j,0)\text{ where $i,j\in{\mathcal{I}}$, $i\geq j$ and $(i+j)<0$}\}.

{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
consists of the
{\mathcal{I}}
-boxes strictly above the principal diagonale,
{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
consists of the
{\mathcal{I}}
-boxes on the principal diagonale, while
{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
consists of the
{\mathcal{I}}
-boxes strictly below the principal diagonale. Note that if
b\in{\mathcal{E}}
, then
\langle v_{0}^{s}(b),v_{0}^{s}(\tau(b))\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=1
for any
1\leq s\leq c(b)
.

The equation (
2
) is equivalent to the equations:

(5)

\xi_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}+\xi_{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}^{(b,j)}=0\quad\text{ if $b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}$, $1\leq j\leq c(b)$ and $1\leq j^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})$.}

Order the basis
{\mathcal{B}}_{0}
such that the first elements are the basis vectors of the form
v_{0}^{j}(b)
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
and
1\leq j\leq c(b)
, the following elements are the basis vectors of the form
v_{0}^{j}(b)
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
and
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and finally the last elements are the basis vectors of the form
v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b))
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
by keeping the same order of the first basis elements. Relative to this ordered basis, the linear transformation
Z_{0}
as above has the form

\begin{pmatrix}A_{11}&A_{12}&A_{13}\\
A_{21}&A_{22}&-A_{12}^{T}\\
A_{31}&-A_{21}^{T}&-A_{11}^{T}\end{pmatrix}\quad\text{$A_{13}=-A_{13}^{T}$, $A_{22}=-A_{22}^{T}$ and $A_{31}=-A_{31}^{T}$, }

where
A_{11}
,
A_{13}
,
A_{31}
are matrices of order
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}\times\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}
,
A_{21}
is a matrix of order
\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}\times\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}
,
A_{12}
is a matrix of order
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}\times\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}
and
A_{22}
is a matrix of order
\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}\times\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}
. Here we are using the same notation as in proposition
4.12
for
\delta^{\prime\prime}_{0}
and
\delta^{\prime\prime\prime}_{0}
.

We will define linear transformations
Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}:V_{0}\rightarrow V_{0}
when
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}
,
1\leq j\leq c(b)
and
1\leq j^{\prime}\leq c(b^{\prime})
for six cases:

(i)

either
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
or
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
;

(ii)

either
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0},b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
or
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0},b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
;

(iii)

either
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0},b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
or
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0},b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
;

(iv)

(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})\neq(\tau(b),j)
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
and
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
;

(v)

(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})\neq(\tau(b),j)
with
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
;

(vi)

(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})\neq(\tau(b),j)
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
and
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
.

Case (i): If either
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
or
b,b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
, we define

Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(b,j)$;}\\
-v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b)),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

This means

\xi_{(b_{1},j_{1})}^{(b_{2},j_{2})}=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(b,j)$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
-1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(b,j)$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We get easily that

\langle Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0

for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
from the equations (
5
). Note that the pair
\tau(b^{\prime}),\tau(b)
falls also in the case (i) and it is easy to see that
Z_{(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})}^{(\tau(b),j)}=-Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
by evaluating both sides on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
.

By looking on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}=(h_{0}(b^{\prime})-h_{0}(b))Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}.

We have used lemma
2.14
(a) in the proof of this formula.

Case (ii): If either
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
or
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
, we define

Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(b,j)$;}\\
-v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b)),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

This means

\xi_{(b_{1},j_{1})}^{(b_{2},j_{2})}=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(b,j)$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
-1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(b,j)$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We get easily that

\langle Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0

for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
from the equations (
5
). Note that the pair
\tau(b^{\prime}),\tau(b)
falls also in the case (ii) and it is easy to see that
Z_{(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})}^{(\tau(b),j)}=-Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
by evaluating both sides on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
.

By looking on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}=(h_{0}(b^{\prime})-h_{0}(b))Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}.

We have used lemma
2.14
(a) in the proof of this formula.

Case (iii): If either
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
or
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
, we define

Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(b,j)$;}\\
-v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b)),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

This means

\xi_{(b_{1},j_{1})}^{(b_{2},j_{2})}=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(b,j)$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
-1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(b,j)$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We get easily that

\langle Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0

for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
from the equations (
5
). Note that the pair
\tau(b^{\prime}),\tau(b)
falls also in the case (iii) and it is easy to see that
Z_{(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})}^{(\tau(b),j)}=-Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
by evaluating both sides on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
.

By looking on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}=(h_{0}(b^{\prime})-h_{0}(b))Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}.

We have used lemma
2.14
(a) in the proof of this formula.

Case (iv): If
(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})\neq(\tau(b),j)
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
, we define

Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(b,j)$;}\\
-v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b)),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

This means

\xi_{(b_{1},j_{1})}^{(b_{2},j_{2})}=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(b,j)$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
-1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(b,j)$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We get easily that

\langle Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0

for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
from the equations (
5
). Note that the pair
\tau(b^{\prime}),\tau(b)
falls also in the case (iv) and it is easy to see that
Z_{(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})}^{(\tau(b),j)}=-Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
by evaluating both sides on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
.

By looking on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}=(h_{0}(b^{\prime})-h_{0}(b))Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}.

We have used lemma
2.14
(a) in the proof of this formula.

Case (v): If
(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})\neq(\tau(b),j)
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{=0}
, we define

Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime}))=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}v_{0}^{j^{\prime}}(b^{\prime}),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(b,j)$;}\\
-v_{0}^{j}(\tau(b)),&\text{ if $(b^{\prime\prime},j^{\prime\prime})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

This means

\xi_{(b_{1},j_{1})}^{(b_{2},j_{2})}=\begin{cases}{\phantom{-}}1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(b,j)$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$;}\\
-1,&\text{ if $(b_{1},j_{1})=(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})$ and $(b_{2},j_{2})=(b,j)$;}\\
{\phantom{-}}0,&\text{otherwise.}\end{cases}

We get easily that

\langle Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v),v^{\prime}\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}+\langle v,Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}(v^{\prime})\rangle_{\mathbf{c}}=0

for all
v,v^{\prime}\in V_{0}
from the equations (
5
). Note that the pair
\tau(b^{\prime}),\tau(b)
falls also in the case (v) and it is easy to see that
Z_{(\tau(b^{\prime}),j^{\prime})}^{(\tau(b),j)}=-Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}
by evaluating both sides on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
.

By looking on the basis vectors
v_{0}^{j^{\prime\prime}}(b^{\prime\prime})
, we have that

ad(H_{\mathbf{c}}|_{V_{0}})Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}=(h_{0}(b^{\prime})-h_{0}(b))Z_{(b,j)}^{(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})}.

We have used lemma
2.14
(a) in the proof of this formula.

Case (vi): If
(b^{\prime},j^{\prime})\neq(\tau(b),j)
with
b\in{\mathcal{E}}^{>0}
,
b^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{E}}^{<0}
, we define
